        # If current player has cards, they can choose to play or pass
        
        best_value = float('-inf') if current_player == 0 else float('inf')
        
        # Option 1: Pass
        # Calculate immediate scores from passing
        score_p1 = 0
        score_p2 = 0
        
        for i, (val, origin) in enumerate(stack):
            if val > 0:  # scoring card
                if origin == 0:  # player 1
                    blocked = False

                            blocked = True

                    if not blocked:

        next_turn = game_state(1 - current_player, p1_state, p2_state, ())

        # But next_turn already includes the scores from the opponent's turn

        # This is for the pass option
        
        pass_value = (score_p1 - score_p2) + next_turn
        
        # Option 2: Play top card
        if len(p1_state) > 0 and current_player == 0:
            top_card = p1_state[0]

            value = game_state(1, new_p1_state, p2_state, new_stack)
            best_value = max(best_value, value)
        elif len(p2_state) > 0 and current_player == 1:

            best_value = min(best_value, value)
        
        # For player who must pass (no cards), they must pass
        # For player who has cards, they choose the best option
        
        # Actually, if current player has no cards, they must pass, so only one option
        
        # If current player has cards, they have a choice between playing or passing
        
        # Return best_value
        
        return best_value

    # If they have cards, they can choose to play or pass, and choose the option that maximizes/minimizes (P1 - P2)
    
    # Player 1 (maximizing) wants to maximize (P1 - P2)
    # Player 2 (minimizing) wants to minimize (P1 - P2) or maximize (P2 - P1) = -(P1 - P2)
    
    # So for player 1: max over actions of (value)
    # For player 2: min over actions of (value)
    
    # Let me implement it properly
    
    def calculate_score(stack):
        # Given stack of (value, origin) tuples, calculate score for player 1 and player 2
        score_1 = 0
        score_2 = 0
        for i, (val, origin) in enumerate(stack):
            if val > 0:
                if origin == 0:
                    # Check if there's any opponent jamming card above it
                    blocked = False
                    for j in range(i):
                        if stack[j][1] == 1 and stack[j][0] == -1:
                            blocked = True
                            break
                    if not blocked:
                        score_1 += val
                elif origin == 1:
                    blocked = False
                    for j in range(i):
                        if stack[j][1] == 0 and stack[j][0] == -1:
                            blocked = True
                            break
                    if not blocked:
                        score_2 += val
        return score_1, score_2
    
    @lru_cache(maxsize=None)
    def game_value(player, p1_remaining, p2_remaining, stack):
        # player: 0 for player 1, 1 for player 2
        # p1_remaining: tuple of remaining cards in p1 deck (from top)
        # p2_remaining: tuple of remaining cards in p2 deck (from top)
        # stack: tuple of (value, origin) where origin is 0 or 1
        
        # If no cards left in either deck and stack is empty, game over
        if len(p1_remaining) == 0 and len(p2_remaining) == 0 and len(stack) == 0:
            return 0
            
        # If current player has no cards, they must pass
        if len(p1_remaining) == 0 and player == 0:
            # Must pass
            score_1, score_2 = calculate_score(stack)
            # After passing, stack is cleared, and it's opponent's turn
            # Net difference: (score_1 - score_2) + (value from next turn)
            next_val = game_value(1, p1_remaining, p2_remaining, ())
            return (score_1 - score_2) + next_val
            
        if len(p2_remaining) == 0 and player == 1:
            # Must pass
            score_1, score_2 = calculate_score(stack)
            next_val = game_value(0, p1_remaining, p2_remaining, ())
            return (score_1 - score_2) + next_val
            
        # Current player has cards, can choose to play or pass
        
        # Option 1: Pass
        score_1, score_2 = calculate_score(stack)
        pass_value = (score_1 - score_2) + game_value(1 - player, p1_remaining, p2_remaining, ())
        
        # Option 2: Play top card
        if player == 0:
            card_value = p1_remaining[0]
            new_p1_remaining = p1_remaining[1:]
            new_stack = stack + ((card_value, 0),)
            play_value = game_value(1, new_p1_remaining, p2_remaining, new_stack)
            # Player 1 wants to maximize (P1 - P2)
            return max(pass_value, play_value)
        else:
            card_value = p2_remaining[0]
            new_p2_remaining = p2_remaining[1:]
            new_stack = stack + ((card_value, 1),)
            play_value = game_value(0, p1_remaining, new_p2_remaining, new_stack)
            # Player 2 wants to minimize (P1 - P2)
            return min(pass_value, play_value)
    
    # Initial state
    result = game_value(0, tuple(a), tuple(b), ())
    print(result)

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

D --Invisible

Problem Statement

You are trying to play a card game called "Invisible" with your friends. This card game uses two types of cards, a "scoring card" and a "jamming card". A positive value is written on each score card. The rules of this card game are as follows.

* The game is played by two players, player 1 and player 2. The game starts on player 1's turn.
* There is one stack and two decks in the field. The stack consists of cards placed by two players. In addition, the deck possessed by each player consists of the score card and the obstruction card possessed by that player. Players can check the order of cards in themselves or in their opponent's deck at any time. There are no cards on the stack at the beginning of the game.
* The two players alternately perform one of the following two actions exactly once.
* Put the top card of your deck on the top of the stack. However, this action cannot be performed when there are no cards in your deck.
* Pass your turn.
* When the player passes the turn, the following processing is performed.
* Each player gets all the scoring cards in the stack that meet the following two conditions. The score card obtained is removed from the field.
1. This is the score card you put on the stack.
2. Above any disturbing cards placed by your opponent (when there are no disturbing cards in your stack, the player gets all the cards he or she puts on the stack).
* Remove all cards in the stack.



If both players pass in succession with no cards on the stack, the game ends. The final score of each player is the sum of the numbers written on the score card obtained by each player.

Each player takes the best action to maximize the value obtained by subtracting the opponent's score from his own score. Your job is to calculate the difference between Player 1's score and Player 2's score when each player behaves optimally for each player's deck given.

Input

The input consists of a single test case of the form:

$ n $ $ m $
$ a_1 $ $ a_2 $ $ \ dots $ $ a_n $
$ b_1 $ $ b_2 $ $ \ dots $ $ b_m $

The first line consists of positive integers $ n $, $ m $ ($ 1 \ le n, m \ le 50 $) representing the number of decks. The second line consists of $ n $ integers, where $ a_i $ represents the $ i $ th card from the top of player 1's deck ($ 1 \ le i \ le n $). $ a_i $ is more than $ 1 $, less than $ 1 {,} 000 {,} 000 $, or $ -1 $. The third line consists of $ m $ integers, where $ b_j $ represents the $ j $ th card from the top of player 2's deck ($ 1 \ le j \ le m $). $ b_j $ is more than $ 1 $, less than $ 1 {,} 000 {,} 000 $, or $ -1 $. When $ a_i $ and $ b_j $ are positive integers, it represents a scoring card, and when it is $ -1 $, it represents a jamming card.

Output

Output (Score of Player 1)-(Score of Player 2) when each player behaves optimally.

Sample Input 1


twenty two
100 -1
200 300

Output for the Sample Input 1


-100

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. Two players (Player 1 and Player 2) take turns starting with Player 1
2. Each player has a deck of cards (scoring cards > 0, jamming cards = -1)
3. On each turn, a player can either:
   - Play the top card of their deck on the stack, or
   - Pass their turn
4. When a player passes, the following happens:
   - Each player gets all scoring cards in the stack that meet:
     - Are their own cards (the ones they played)
     - Are above any opponent's jamming cards
   - All cards in the stack are removed
5. The game ends when both players pass consecutively with no cards on stack
6. Goal: Calculate (Player 1's score) - (Player 2's score) when both play optimally

## Key Insights

1. This is a minimax game where players want to maximize their own score relative to opponent's score
2. The state can be defined by (player_turn, deck1_remaining, deck2_remaining, stack)
3. When a player passes, the scoring rule is crucial: a player gets their scoring cards that are not "blocked" by opponent's jamming cards
4. Jamming cards (-1) block scoring cards below them in the stack

## Strategy Development

The game tree needs to consider:
- Current player's turn
- Current state of both decks
- Current stack state

For each state, we need to consider both options:
1. Play the top card (if available)
2. Pass (if applicable)

When passing, we calculate the score gain for both players based on the current stack configuration.

## Implementation Approach

I'll use dynamic programming with memoization to solve this game tree. The state will be:
- Current player (0 or 1)
- Remaining cards in player 1's deck (as a tuple)
- Remaining cards in player 2's deck (as a tuple)
- Current stack (as a tuple)

For each state, I'll compute the best outcome for the current player.

Let me trace through the first example:
- Player 1: [100, -1]
- Player 2: [200, 300]

Player 1 starts. They can either play 100 or pass.

If Player 1 plays 100:
- Stack becomes [100]
- Player 2's turn

Player 2 can play 200 or pass.

If Player 2 plays 200:
- Stack becomes [100, 200]
- Player 1's turn

Player 1 can play -1 (jamming card) or pass.

If Player 1 plays -1:
- Stack becomes [100, 200, -1]
- Player 2's turn

Player 2 can play 300 or pass.

If Player 2 plays 300:
- Stack becomes [100, 200, -1, 300]
- Player 1's turn

- Player 1 gets cards above Player 2's jamming cards? Wait, let me re-read.

Actually, when a player passes, they get their scoring cards that are above any opponent's jamming cards.

So when stack is [100, 200, -1, 300], and it's Player 1's turn, Player 1 passes:
- Player 1 gets their own scoring cards that are above any of Player 2's jamming cards
- Player 2's jamming cards are none (Player 2 played 200, 300 - both scoring cards)
- So Player 1 gets 100
- Player 2 gets 200, 300
- Stack is cleared

Wait, I think I misunderstood. Let me re-read.

When a player passes, each player gets all scoring cards in the stack that meet:
1. This is the score card you put on the stack (your own cards)
2. Above any disturbing cards placed by your opponent (when there are no disturbing cards in your stack, the player gets all the cards he or she put on the stack)

This means: when Player 1 passes, Player 1 gets their scoring cards that are not blocked by Player 2's jamming cards (if any), and similarly for Player 2.

But jamming cards block scoring cards below them (in the stack).

So if stack is [100, 200, -1, 300]:
- The -1 is Player 2's jamming card at position 2
- 300 (Player 2's scoring card) is below it, so it's blocked and not obtained by Player 2
- 100 (Player 1's scoring card) is above it, so it's obtained by Player 1
- 200 (Player 2's scoring card) is below it, so it's not obtained by Player 1 (since it's Player 2's card) but is it obtained by Player 2?

Actually, the card must be your own card and be above any opponent's jamming card.

So:
- Player 1 gets: 100 (Player 1's card, above Player 2's jamming card at position 2)
- Player 2 gets: 200 (Player 2's card, but is it above any jamming card? 200 is at position 1, before the jamming card at position 2, so it's above the jamming card)
- 300 is Player 2's card but below jamming card (at position 2), so it's blocked and not obtained

The rule says "above any disturbing cards" - so if a card is below a jamming card, it's blocked.

So 200 is above the jamming card at position 2 (since it's before), so it should be obtained by Player 2.

Let me reconsider: "above any disturbing cards" means cards that are positioned above (higher in stack order) any opponent's jamming cards.

So in [100, 200, -1, 300]:
- Position 0: 100 (Player 1)
- Position 1: 200 (Player 2) 
- Position 2: -1 (Player 2 - jamming card)
- Position 3: 300 (Player 2)

For Player 1: gets 100 (since 100 is above the jamming card at position 2)
For Player 2: gets 200 (200 is at position 1, which is above position 2) and 300 (300 is at position 3, which is below position 2, so blocked)

So Player 1 gets 100, Player 2 gets 200.

This seems wrong because the game says "the score card obtained is removed from the field" and both players get their own scoring cards that are above opponent's jamming cards.

I think I need to implement a proper simulation.

```python
    @lru_cache(maxsize=None)
    def game(turn, p1, p2, stack):
        # turn: 0 or 1

    def get_scores_from_stack(stack, p1, p2):
        # stack: tuple of (card_value, player)
        # p1, p2: tuples of remaining cards
        # Returns (p1_score, p2_score)
```

Wait, I see a critical bug. In the scoring function, I'm not correctly handling the "above" relationship. If a card is at position i and there's a jamming card at position j, then position i is before j if i < j, so it's above it.

However, the column in the stack is from top to bottom, so position 0 is top, position 1 is below it, etc.

So a card at position i is above a card at position j if i < j.

So for a scoring card at position i to be above a jamming card at position j, we need i < j.

If there are multiple jamming cards, a card is above them if it's above the highest jamming card? Or above any jamming card?

The rule says "above any disturbing cards" - so if it's above any jamming card, it is obtained.

Actually, if a card is above a jamming card, it is blocked from being obtained by the opponent. But the rule says "above any disturbing cards" - so if a card is above at least one jamming card, then it might be obtained.

Let me re-read: "above any disturbing cards placed by your opponent" - the phrasing is ambiguous.

But in the example, in [100, 200, -1, 300]:
- 100 is at position 0, there's a jamming card at position 2, so 0 < 2, so 100 is above it, so Player 1 gets 100
- 200 is at position 1, 1 < 2, so it's above the jamming card, so Player 2 gets 200
- 300 is at position 3, 3 > 2, so it's below the jamming card, so it's not obtained by anyone

This matches.

For a scoring card to be obtained by its owner, it must be above any opponent's jamming card. So if it's above at least one jamming card, it is obtained.

But "above any disturbing cards" could mean "above every disturbing card" or "above at least one disturbing card".

Given that in the example, only cards above the jamming card are obtained, and the ones below are blocked, I believe it means: a card is obtained if it is above at least one jamming card.

But actually, if a card is above a jamming card, it should be blocked from being obtained by the opponent.

The rule says: "above any disturbing cards placed by your opponent" - this likely means the card is above the disturbing card, so if it's above any disturbing card, then for the player, they get it.

I think it means: the card is obtained if it is positioned above any jamming card from the opponent.

So in the example, 100 is above the jamming card, so Player 1 gets it.

200 is above the jamming card, so Player 2 gets it.

300 is below, so not obtained.

This matches.

So my implementation of the scoring function is correct.

Let me fix the code and submit.

```python
from functools import lru_cache

def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    a = list(map(int, input().split()))
    b = list(map(int, input().split()))
    
    @lru_cache(maxsize=None)
    def get_scores_from_stack(stack, p1, p2):
        # stack: tuple of (card_value, player) where player is 0 for P1, 1 for P2
        # p1, p2: tuples of remaining cards (for reference, but we don't use them in scoring)
        p1_score = 0
        p2_score = 0
        
        # Find all jamming card positions in the stack
        jamming_positions = []
        for i, (card_value, player) in enumerate(stack):
            if card_value == -1:
                jamming_positions.append(i)
        
        # For each scoring card in the stack
        for i, (card_value, player) in enumerate(stack):
            if card_value > 0:  # scoring card
                # Check if this card is above any jamming card
                # A card at position i is above a jamming card at position j if i < j
                is_above_any_jamming = any(i < j for j in jamming_positions)
                
                if is_above_any_jamming:
                    if player == 0:
                        p1_score += card_value
                    else:
                        p2_score += card_value
        
        return p1_score, p2_score
    
    @lru_cache(maxsize=None)
    def game(turn, p1_remaining, p2_remaining, stack):
        # turn: 0 for Player 1's turn, 1 for Player 2's turn
        # p1_remaining, p2_remaining: tuples of remaining cards
        # stack: tuple of (card_value, player_id) pairs
        
        # If no cards left in either player's deck and no stack, game ends
        if len(p1_remaining) == 0 and len(p2_remaining) == 0 and len(stack) == 0:
            return 0
            
        # If current player has no cards, they must pass
        if turn == 0 and len(p1_remaining) == 0:
            # Pass - scoring happens
            p1_score, p2_score = get_scores_from_stack(stack, p1_remaining, p2_remaining)
            score_diff = p1_score - p2_score
            # After scoring, stack is cleared
            return score_diff + game(1, p1_remaining, p2_remaining, ())
            
        if turn == 1 and len(p2_remaining) == 0:
            # Pass - scoring happens
            p1_score, p2_score = get_scores_from_stack(stack, p1_remaining, p2_remaining)
            score_diff = p1_score - p2_score
            return score_diff + game(0, p1_remaining, p2_remaining, ())
            
        # Current player can play if they have cards
        if turn == 0:
            if len(p1_remaining) > 0:
                # Play the top card
                card = p1_remaining[0]
                new_p1 = p1_remaining[1:]
                new_stack = stack + ((card, 0),)
                result = game(1, new_p1, p2_remaining, new_stack)
                
                # Pass option
                p1_score, p2_score = get_scores_from_stack(stack, p1_remaining, p2_remaining)
                pass_result = p1_score - p2_score + game(1, p1_remaining, p2_remaining, ())
                
                # Player 1 wants to maximize (their score - opponent's score)
                return max(result, pass_result)
            else:
                # Only pass option
                p1_score, p2_score = get_scores_from_stack(stack, p1_remaining, p2_remaining)
                return p1_score - p2_score + game(1, p1_remaining, p2_remaining, ())
        else:  # turn == 1
            if len(p2_remaining) > 0:
                # Play the top card
                card = p2_remaining[0]
                new_p2 = p2_remaining[1:]
                new_stack = stack + ((card, 1),)
                result = game(0, p1_remaining, new_p2, new_stack)
                
                # Pass option
                p1_score, p2_score = get_scores_from_stack(stack, p1_remaining, p2_remaining)
                pass_result = p1_score - p2_score + game(0, p1_remaining, p2_remaining, ())
                
                # Player 2 wants to minimize (P1's score - P2's score)
                return min(result, pass_result)
            else:
                # Only pass option
                p1_score, p2_score = get_scores_from_stack(stack, p1_remaining, p2_remaining)
                return p1_score - p2_score + game(0, p1_remaining, p2_remaining, ())
                
    # Initial state
    result = game(0, tuple(a), tuple(b), ())
    print(result)

solve()
```

from functools import lru_cache

def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    a = list(map(int, input().split()))
    b = list(map(int, input().split()))
    
    @lru_cache(maxsize=None)
    def get_scores_from_stack(stack, p1, p2):
        # stack: tuple of (card_value, player) where player is 0 for P1, 1 for P2
        # p1, p2: tuples of remaining cards (for reference, but we don't use them in scoring)
        p1_score = 0
        p2_score = 0
        
        # Find all jamming card positions in the stack
        jamming_positions = []
        for i, (card_value, player) in enumerate(stack):
            if card_value == -1:
                jamming_positions.append(i)
        
        # For each scoring card in the stack
        for i, (card_value, player) in enumerate(stack):
            if card_value > 0:  # scoring card
                # Check if this card is above any jamming card
                # A card at position i is above a jamming card at position j if i < j
                is_above_any_jamming = any(i < j for j in jamming_positions)
                
                if is_above_any_jamming:
                    if player == 0:
                        p1_score += card_value
                    else:
                        p2_score += card_value
        
        return p1_score, p2_score
    
    @lru_cache(maxsize=None)
    def game(turn, p1_remaining, p2_remaining, stack):
        # turn: 0 for Player 1's turn, 1 for Player 2's turn
        # p1_remaining, p2_remaining: tuples of remaining cards
        # stack: tuple of (card_value, player_id) pairs
        
        # If no cards left in either player's deck and no stack, game ends
        if len(p1_remaining) == 0 and len(p2_remaining) == 0 and len(stack) == 0:
            return 0
            
        # If current player has no cards, they must pass
        if turn == 0 and len(p1_remaining) == 0:
            # Pass - scoring happens
            p1_score, p2_score = get_scores_from_stack(stack, p1_remaining, p2_remaining)
            score_diff = p1_score - p2_score
            # After scoring, stack is cleared
            return score_diff + game(1, p1_remaining, p2_remaining, ())
            
        if turn == 1 and len(p2_remaining) == 0:
            # Pass - scoring happens
            p1_score, p2_score = get_scores_from_stack(stack, p1_remaining, p2_remaining)
            score_diff = p1_score - p2_score
            return score_diff + game(0, p1_remaining, p2_remaining, ())
            
        # Current player can play if they have cards
        if turn == 0:
            if len(p1_remaining) > 0:
                # Play the top card
                card = p1_remaining[0]
                new_p1 = p1_remaining[1:]
                new_stack = stack + ((card, 0),)
                result = game(1, new_p1, p2_remaining, new_stack)
                
                # Pass option
                p1_score, p2_score = get_scores_from_stack(stack, p1_remaining, p2_remaining)
                pass_result = p1_score - p2_score + game(1, p1_remaining, p2_remaining, ())
                
                # Player 1 wants to maximize (their score - opponent's score)
                return max(result, pass_result)
            else:
                # Only pass option
                p1_score, p2_score = get_scores_from_stack(stack, p1_remaining, p2_remaining)
                return p1_score - p2_score + game(1, p1_remaining, p2_remaining, ())
        else:  # turn == 1
            if len(p2_remaining) > 0:
                # Play the top card
                card = p2_remaining[0]
                new_p2 = p2_remaining[1:]
                new_stack = stack + ((card, 1),)
                result = game(0, p1_remaining, new_p2, new_stack)
                
                # Pass option
                p1_score, p2_score = get_scores_from_stack(stack, p1_remaining, p2_remaining)
                pass_result = p1_score - p2_score + game(0, p1_remaining, p2_remaining, ())
                
                # Player 2 wants to minimize (P1's score - P2's score)
                return min(result, pass_result)
            else:
                # Only pass option
                p1_score, p2_score = get_scores_from_stack(stack, p1_remaining, p2_remaining)
                return p1_score - p2_score + game(0, p1_remaining, p2_remaining, ())
                
    # Initial state
    result = game(0, tuple(a), tuple(b), ())
    print(result)

solve()

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

D --Invisible

Problem Statement

You are trying to play a card game called "Invisible" with your friends. This card game uses two types of cards, a "scoring card" and a "jamming card". A positive value is written on each score card. The rules of this card game are as follows.

* The game is played by two players, player 1 and player 2. The game starts on player 1's turn.
* There is one stack and two decks in the field. The stack consists of cards placed by two players. In addition, the deck possessed by each player consists of the score card and the obstruction card possessed by that player. Players can check the order of cards in themselves or in their opponent's deck at any time. There are no cards on the stack at the beginning of the game.
* The two players alternately perform one of the following two actions exactly once.
* Put the top card of your deck on the top of the stack. However, this action cannot be performed when there are no cards in your deck.
* Pass your turn.
* When the player passes the turn, the following processing is performed.
* Each player gets all the scoring cards in the stack that meet the following two conditions. The score card obtained is removed from the field.
1. This is the score card you put on the stack.
2. Above any disturbing cards placed by your opponent (when there are no disturbing cards in your stack, the player gets all the cards he or she puts on the stack).
* Remove all cards in the stack.



If both players pass in succession with no cards on the stack, the game ends. The final score of each player is the sum of the numbers written on the score card obtained by each player.

Each player takes the best action to maximize the value obtained by subtracting the opponent's score from his own score. Your job is to calculate the difference between Player 1's score and Player 2's score when each player behaves optimally for each player's deck given.

Input

The input consists of a single test case of the form:

$ n $ $ m $
$ a_1 $ $ a_2 $ $ \ dots $ $ a_n $
$ b_1 $ $ b_2 $ $ \ dots $ $ b_m $

The first line consists of positive integers $ n $, $ m $ ($ 1 \ le n, m \ le 50 $) representing the number of decks. The second line consists of $ n $ integers, where $ a_i $ represents the $ i $ th card from the top of player 1's deck ($ 1 \ le i \ le n $). $ a_i $ is more than $ 1 $, less than $ 1 {,} 000 {,} 000 $, or $ -1 $. The third line consists of $ m $ integers, where $ b_j $ represents the $ j $ th card from the top of player 2's deck ($ 1 \ le j \ le m $). $ b_j $ is more than $ 1 $, less than $ 1 {,} 000 {,} 000 $, or $ -1 $. When $ a_i $ and $ b_j $ are positive integers, it represents a scoring card, and when it is $ -1 $, it represents a jamming card.

Output

Output (Score of Player 1)-(Score of Player 2) when each player behaves optimally.

Sample Input 1


twenty two
100 -1
200 300

Output for the Sample Input 1


-100

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. Two players (1 and 2) take turns starting with player 1
2. Each player has a deck with cards that are either:
   - Positive values: scoring cards (gain points)
   - -1: jamming cards (block/oppose)
3. On each turn, a player can either:
   - Put the top card of their deck on the stack, or
   - Pass their turn
4. When a player passes, the following happens:
   - Each player gets all scoring cards in the stack that:
     a) Were placed by them, OR
     b) Are above any jamming cards placed by the opponent
   - All cards in the stack are removed
5. The game ends when both players pass consecutively with no cards on the stack
6. Players play optimally to maximize (their score - opponent's score)

## Key Insights

This is a game theory problem that can be solved with dynamic programming or minimax search. Since the state space is small (n, m ≤ 50), we can use memoization to solve it.

The state can be represented as (p1_deck_remaining, p2_deck_remaining, stack) where:
- p1_deck_remaining is the current state of player 1's deck
- p2_deck_remaining is the current state of player 2's deck  
- stack is the current sequence of cards on the stack

However, since we only care about the relative ordering and which cards are scoring vs jamming, we can simplify.

## Solution Strategy

1. Use recursive DFS with memoization
2. At each state, the current player has two choices:
   - Play their top card (if available)
   - Pass
3. When passing, calculate the score gain for both players based on the current stack
4. Return the optimal difference (player1_score - player2_score)

## Step-by-step Implementation

```python
def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    a = list(map(int, input().split()))
    b = list(map(int, input().split()))
    
    # Memoization dictionary
    memo = {}
    
    def get_score_gain(deck1, deck2, stack):
        # Get score gains when both players pass
        score1 = 0
        score2 = 0
        
        # Process stack - find scoring cards that belong to each player
        # For each player, collect cards that are:
        # 1. Their own card (placed by them), OR
        # 2. Above a jamming card placed by opponent
        
        # We need to process stack from top to bottom
        # Keep track of jamming cards placed by opponent
        # If opponent has jamming card, it blocks all scoring cards below it
        # When we reach a jamming card, everything below it is blocked
        
        # Create a list of all cards with their player and type
        cards = []
        for i, card in enumerate(stack):
            if card != -1:  # scoring card
                if i % 2 == 0:  # even index (player 1's turn)
                    cards.append((card, 1))
                else:  # odd index (player 2's turn)
                    cards.append((card, 2))
            else:  # jamming card
                cards.append((-1, 0))
        
        # Process cards from top to bottom
        # Maintain a flag for whether opponent's jamming card is present
        # Any card after opponent's jamming card is blocked
        active = True  # True means scoring cards are active
        
        score1 = 0
        score2 = 0
        
        for card, player in cards:
            if card == -1:  # jamming card
                active = False  # blocks all future scoring cards
            elif active and card > 0:
                if player == 1:
                    score1 += card
                else:
                    score2 += card
        
        return score1, score2
    
    def minimax(p1_deck, p2_deck, stack, turn):
        # turn: 0 = player 1's turn, 1 = player 2's turn
        # p1_deck, p2_deck: remaining decks (as tuples to make hashable)
        # stack: current stack (as tuple)
        
        state = (tuple(p1_deck), tuple(p2_deck), tuple(stack), turn)
        if state in memo:
            return memo[state]
        
        # If both players have no cards and both pass, game ends
        if len(p1_deck) == 0 and len(p2_deck) == 0:
            # If we reach here, both pass consecutively
            # Calculate final scores from stack
            score1, score2 = get_score_gain(p1_deck, p2_deck, stack)
            memo[state] = score1 - score2
            return score1 - score2
        
        # If current player has no cards, they must pass
        if len(p1_deck) == 0 and turn == 0:
            # Player 1 has no cards, so they pass
            result = minimax(p1_deck, p2_deck, stack, 1)
            memo[state] = result
            return result
        
        if len(p2_deck) == 0 and turn == 1:
            # Player 2 has no cards, so they pass
            result = minimax(p1_deck, p2_deck, stack, 0)
            memo[state] = result
            return result
        
        # Current player can choose to play or pass
        best_result = float('-inf') if turn == 0 else float('inf')
        
        # Option 1: Play top card
        if len(p1_deck) > 0 and turn == 0:
            card = p1_deck[0]
            new_p1_deck = p1_deck[1:]
            new_stack = [card] + stack
            result = minimax(new_p1_deck, p2_deck, new_stack, 1)
            best_result = max(best_result, result)
            
        if len(p2_deck) > 0 and turn == 1:
            card = p2_deck[0]
            new_p2_deck = p2_deck[1:]
            new_stack = [card] + stack
            result = minimax(p1_deck, new_p2_deck, new_stack, 0)
            best_result = min(best_result, result)
        
        # Option 2: Pass
        if turn == 0:
            # Player 1 passes
            score1, score2 = get_score_gain(p1_deck, p2_deck, stack)
            result = score1 - score2
            best_result = max(best_result, result)
            
        if turn == 1:
            # Player 2 passes
            score1, score2 = get_score_gain(p1_deck, p2_deck, stack)
            result = score1 - score2
            best_result = min(best_result, result)
        
        memo[state] = best_result
        return best_result
    
    # Initial state: both players have full decks, empty stack, player 1's turn
    result = minimax(a[:], b[:], [], 0)
    print(result)

solve()
```

```python
    # they get all scoring cards in the stack that are either:
    # 1. Their own cards, OR
    # 2. Above any jamming cards placed by the opponent

    # Let's start over with correct state representation
    
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    a = list(map(int, input().split()))
    b = list(map(int, input().split()))
    
    memo = {}
    
    def get_score_from_stack(stack):
        # stack is list of tuples (value, player) where player is 1 or 2
        # Returns (score1, score2)
        score1 = 0
        score2 = 0
        
        # Process from top to bottom
        opponent_jamming = False
        
        for value, player in stack:
            if value == -1:  # jamming card
                opponent_jamming = True
            elif value > 0:  # scoring card
                if not opponent_jamming:
                    if player == 1:
                        score1 += value
                    else:
                        score2 += value
        
        return score1, score2
    
    def play_game(p1_deck, p2_deck, stack, turn):
        # p1_deck, p2_deck: lists of remaining cards
        # stack: list of (card_value, player) - player is 1 or 2
        # turn: 0 for player 1, 1 for player 2
        
        key = (tuple(p1_deck), tuple(p2_deck), tuple(stack), turn)
        if key in memo:
            return memo[key]
        
        # If both players have no cards
        if len(p1_deck) == 0 and len(p2_deck) == 0:
            score1, score2 = get_score_from_stack(stack)
            result = score1 - score2
            memo[key] = result
            return result
        
        # If it's player 1's turn
        if turn == 0:
            # Player 1 can either play or pass
            options = []
            
            # Option 1: Play top card of p1_deck
            if p1_deck:
                card = p1_deck[0]
                new_p1_deck = p1_deck[1:]
                new_stack = stack + [(card, 1)]
                result = play_game(new_p1_deck, p2_deck, new_stack, 1)
                options.append(result)
            
            # Option 2: Pass
            score1, score2 = get_score_from_stack(stack)
            result = score1 - score2
            options.append(result)
            
            # Choose the option that maximizes (my score - opponent's score)
            best_result = max(options)
            memo[key] = best_result
            return best_result
        else:
            # Player 2's turn (turn = 1)
            options = []
            
            # Option 1: Play top card of p2_deck
            if p2_deck:
                card = p2_deck[0]
                new_p2_deck = p2_deck[1:]
                new_stack = stack + [(card, 2)]
                result = play_game(p1_deck, new_p2_deck, new_stack, 0)
                options.append(result)
            
            # Option 2: Pass
            score1, score2 = get_score_from_stack(stack)
            result = score1 - score2
            options.append(result)
            
            # Choose the option that maximizes (my score - opponent's score)
            # But since player 2 is trying to maximize (his score - opponent's score),
            # and we're calculating (p1_score - p2_score), player 2 will choose to minimize (p1_score - p2_score)
            # which means we want to maximize (p2_score - p1_score)
            # So we need to negate the score in player 2's turn
            
            # Wait, no: our function returns (p1_score - p2_score)
            # When player 2 passes, we get (p1_score - p2_score)
            # When player 2 plays, we get the result of the next state
            # Player 2 wants to maximize (p2_score - p1_score) = -(p1_score - p2_score)
            # So player 2 will choose the option that minimizes (p1_score - p2_score)
            
            best_result = min(options)
            memo[key] = best_result
            return best_result
    
    result = play_game(a[:], b[:], [], 0)
    print(result)
```

                if card > 0:
                    # We need to know who placed this card - we don't have that info
                    # This is a problem
                    pass

            # The stack should store (card, player) pairs

        memo[key] = 0
        return memo[key]

    # After reading the problem again, I think the key insight is that when a player passes,
    # they get all scoring cards in the stack that are either:
    # 1. Their own cards, OR
    # 2. Above any jamming cards placed by the opponent
    
    # So I need to know who placed each card in the stack
    
    # I'll represent the stack as a list of (card_value, player) where player is 1 or 2
    # But I also need to know if it's a scoring card or jamming card
    
    # Let's start over with correct state representation
    
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    a = list(map(int, input().split()))
    b = list(map(int, input().split()))
    
    memo = {}
    
    def get_score_from_stack(stack):
        # stack is list of tuples (value, player) where player is 1 or 2
        # Returns (score1, score2)
        score1 = 0
        score2 = 0
        
        # Process from top to bottom
        opponent_jamming = False
        
        for value, player in stack:
            if value == -1:  # jamming card
                opponent_jamming = True
            elif value > 0:  # scoring card
                if not opponent_jamming:
                    if player == 1:
                        score1 += value
                    else:
                        score2 += value
        
        return score1, score2
    
    def play_game(p1_deck, p2_deck, stack, turn):
        # p1_deck, p2_deck: lists of remaining cards
        # stack: list of (card_value, player) - player is 1 or 2
        # turn: 0 for player 1, 1 for player 2
        
        key = (tuple(p1_deck), tuple(p2_deck), tuple(stack), turn)
        if key in memo:
            return memo[key]
        
        # If both players have no cards
        if len(p1_deck) == 0 and len(p2_deck) == 0:
            score1, score2 = get_score_from_stack(stack)
            result = score1 - score2
            memo[key] = result
            return result
        
        # If it's player 1's turn
        if turn == 0:
            # Player 1 can either play or pass
            options = []
            
            # Option 1: Play top card of p1_deck
            if p1_deck:
                card = p1_deck[0]
                new_p1_deck = p1_deck[1:]
                new_stack = stack + [(card, 1)]
                result = play_game(new_p1_deck, p2_deck, new_stack, 1)
                options.append(result)
            
            # Option 2: Pass
            score1, score2 = get_score_from_stack(stack)
            result = score1 - score2
            options.append(result)
            
            # Choose the option that maximizes (my score - opponent's score)
            best_result = max(options)
            memo[key] = best_result
            return best_result
        else:
            # Player 2's turn (turn = 1)
            options = []
            
            # Option 1: Play top card of p2_deck
            if p2_deck:
                card = p2_deck[0]
                new_p2_deck = p2_deck[1:]
                new_stack = stack + [(card, 2)]
                result = play_game(p1_deck, new_p2_deck, new_stack, 0)
                options.append(result)
            
            # Option 2: Pass
            score1, score2 = get_score_from_stack(stack)
            result = score1 - score2
            options.append(result)
            
            # Choose the option that maximizes (my score - opponent's score)
            # But since player 2 is trying to maximize (his score - opponent's score),
            # and we're calculating (p1_score - p2_score), player 2 will choose to minimize (p1_score - p2_score)
            # which means we want to maximize (p2_score - p1_score)
            # So we need to negate the score in player 2's turn
            
            # Wait, no: our function returns (p1_score - p2_score)
            # When player 2 passes, we get (p1_score - p2_score)
            # When player 2 plays, we get the result of the next state
            # Player 2 wants to maximize (p2_score - p1_score) = -(p1_score - p2_score)
            # So player 2 will choose the option that minimizes (p1_score - p2_score)
            
            best_result = min(options)
            memo[key] = best_result
            return best_result
    
    result = play_game(a[:], b[:], [], 0)
    print(result)

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

D --Invisible

Problem Statement

You are trying to play a card game called "Invisible" with your friends. This card game uses two types of cards, a "scoring card" and a "jamming card". A positive value is written on each score card. The rules of this card game are as follows.

* The game is played by two players, player 1 and player 2. The game starts on player 1's turn.
* There is one stack and two decks in the field. The stack consists of cards placed by two players. In addition, the deck possessed by each player consists of the score card and the obstruction card possessed by that player. Players can check the order of cards in themselves or in their opponent's deck at any time. There are no cards on the stack at the beginning of the game.
* The two players alternately perform one of the following two actions exactly once.
* Put the top card of your deck on the top of the stack. However, this action cannot be performed when there are no cards in your deck.
* Pass your turn.
* When the player passes the turn, the following processing is performed.
* Each player gets all the scoring cards in the stack that meet the following two conditions. The score card obtained is removed from the field.
1. This is the score card you put on the stack.
2. Above any disturbing cards placed by your opponent (when there are no disturbing cards in your stack, the player gets all the cards he or she puts on the stack).
* Remove all cards in the stack.



If both players pass in succession with no cards on the stack, the game ends. The final score of each player is the sum of the numbers written on the score card obtained by each player.

Each player takes the best action to maximize the value obtained by subtracting the opponent's score from his own score. Your job is to calculate the difference between Player 1's score and Player 2's score when each player behaves optimally for each player's deck given.

Input

The input consists of a single test case of the form:

$ n $ $ m $
$ a_1 $ $ a_2 $ $ \ dots $ $ a_n $
$ b_1 $ $ b_2 $ $ \ dots $ $ b_m $

The first line consists of positive integers $ n $, $ m $ ($ 1 \ le n, m \ le 50 $) representing the number of decks. The second line consists of $ n $ integers, where $ a_i $ represents the $ i $ th card from the top of player 1's deck ($ 1 \ le i \ le n $). $ a_i $ is more than $ 1 $, less than $ 1 {,} 000 {,} 000 $, or $ -1 $. The third line consists of $ m $ integers, where $ b_j $ represents the $ j $ th card from the top of player 2's deck ($ 1 \ le j \ le m $). $ b_j $ is more than $ 1 $, less than $ 1 {,} 000 {,} 000 $, or $ -1 $. When $ a_i $ and $ b_j $ are positive integers, it represents a scoring card, and when it is $ -1 $, it represents a jamming card.

Output

Output (Score of Player 1)-(Score of Player 2) when each player behaves optimally.

twenty two
100 -1
200 300

Output for the Sample Input 1


-100
